الكتاب: مرجع مذاكرة مكثّف مرافق لمنهج البكالوريا (مفردات + نصوص + بنوك أسئلة + قواعد).
التوزيع: مؤسسة الكوتر بالفجالة — القاهرة (شارع الوزير علاء الدين).
للتواصل: 010 22 50 66 90 — 012 25 43 31 03 — giants.books.series

دليل الرموز:
JRE = استراتيجية الإجابة (Justify التبرير - Reason السبب - Evidence الدليل).
[SB] / SBR = سؤال وارد في كتاب المدرسة (Student Book).

ملاحظة: النص منظم ومُنقَّح من نسخة ممسوحة ضوئيًا (OCR)؛ وقد تظهر أخطاء يسيرة متفرقة،
خاصة في الجداول ثنائية اللغة. الرواية هنا هي نسخة مبسّطة من رواية The Lost World.

Read this post carefully:

Some teenagers enjoy creative activities such as painting, writing, and playing music.
These activities can help them express their feelings and reduce stress. For example, a
student may feel more relaxed after soending some time drawing or listening to music.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

Do you think creative activities are important for teenagers?

Give your claim and support it with reasons and evidence from the text.

Yes, I think creative activities are important for teenagers.
This is because they help teenagers express their feelings and reduce
stress.

For example, the text says that drawing or listening to music can make
a student feel more relaxed.

How Social Media is Reshaping the Way Teenagers See Themselves:

Over the past decade, social media platforms have transformed
from simple communication tools into carefully chosen stages where
individuals perform carefully constructed versions of their lives. For
teenagers, who are at a particularly sensitive stage of identity
formation, this change carries significant psychological results.
Research published by the American Psychological Association in
2023 found that teenagers who spent more than three hours daily on
image-based platforms such as Instagram and TikTok were twice as
likely to report symptoms of low self-esteem and body dissatisfaction
compared to those who used social media for less than one hour per
day. What makes social media especially powerful, and especially
dangerous, is the mechanism of social comparison. Unlike traditional media, where the gap
between ordinary people and celebrities is obvious, social media has hidden this gap
completely. Teenagers are now comparing themselves not only to distant celebrities, but to
classmates, acquaintances, and influencers who present their lives as easily perfect.
Psychologists use the term “highlight real effect" to describe this phenomenon. What appears
online is a selective, filtered, and strategically timed part of reality, yet viewers instinctively

treat it as a complete and accurate picture.

A. Answer these questions.
1. Identify:
According to the text, how have social media platforms changed over the past decade?

2. Identify:
What percentage of teenagers who spend more than three hours a day on image-
based social media platforms report symptoms of low self-esteem?

3. Identify:
What term do sieht use to describe the selective posting of perfect moments?

B. Answer in full sentences.

1. Analyze:
Why does the text describe social media as “highly curated stages”? What does this
imply about what we see online?

2. موا
According to the text, why is social comparison more dangerous on social media than
in traditional media?

3. Evaluate:
The text states that teenagers are “twice as likely” to report symptoms of low self-
esteem. How convincing is this statistic? What additional information would you need?

4. Analyze:
Why does the text end with a discussion of media literacy?
What message is the mar trying to convey?

1. Constant social comparison made Ahmed feel that his life was less successful than the
lives he saw online. What does social comparison mean in this context? SERA
a. Judging yourself by comparing your life with other people's lives.

b. Discussing social problems with friends.
c. Studying the traditions of different societies.
d. Sharing personal information online.

2. A podcast host invited only guests who agreed with his views and dismissed opposing
opinions without discussing them fairly. Listeners described the program as biased.
Which situation is the clearest example of being “biased”? SBR
a. A judge scores every performance according to the same published rules.

b. A judge gives a friend a higher score even though another performer clearly did better.
c. Ajudge asks another official to review a result that is extremely close.
d. A judge changes a score after noticing a genuine mistake in the first calculation.

3. Mariam posts only photos of holidays, celebrations, and achievements, so her
followers imagine that her life is always perfect. This creates a highlight-ree! effect.

Which situation is the clearest example of the “highlight-reel effect”?

a. A student shares both successes and failures during a difficult school year.

b. A traveller posts only luxury hotels and exciting trips while hiding stressful moments.
c. A teacher uploads full lessons so students can review their mistakes.

d. A family discusses both positive and negative events openly at home.

4. The writer used biased language such as “terrible” and “completely useless” when
describing the plan. What does biased language mean in this context?

a. Words used only in scientific reports.
b. Words that show a strong opinion and are not fair or neutral.
c. Words that make information easier to memorize.
d. Words used to introduce several different viewpoints.
5. A news report should use neutral language when presenting different sides of an
argument. What does neutral language mean in this context?
a. Language that strongly supports one side.
b. Language that deliberately hides important facts.
c. Language that is balanced, objective, and free from bias.
d. Language that contains emotional and exaggerated expressions.

Find the word or phrase in the text that could meaningfully replace the words below:

When reading online content, students should notice how language can influence their
opinions. A partial article may support one side unfairly, while an impartial report presents
information fairly. Careful writers often use cautious language when they are not
completely certain about a claim. Social media can also create a selective positive
portrayal, showing mainly successful or attractive moments. This may encourage
comparing oneself with others. Writers sometimes use a judgmental adjective to express
an opinion or a question that does not require an answer to influence readers. A
strengthening word can make a statement sound more powerful. All these messages may
eventually affect a young person's self-respect.

Social media platforms often promote an image-based culture where influencers are)

An Example of Persuasive Writing Techniques €

Do we really need to spend most of our free time sitting in front of screens? Our
daily choices can have an extremely important effect on our physical and emotional
well-being. Regular exercise is absolutely essential for a healthy lifestyle. It strengthens
the body, reduces stress, and always gives us a good reason to become more active.

However, improving our well-being does not necessarily mean making huge
_ changes. Even small steps may make a difference. Walking for thirty minutes, eating a
very balanced diet, and spending quality time with family and friends could possibly
improve both our physical and mental health. Who wouldn't want to feel healthier and
I happier every day?

Therefore, we should never underestimate the importance of our daily habits. A
healthier lifestyle is really worth the effort, and everyone can probably find a simple
way to make a positive change.

Biased language = opinion / judgment/ stereotype لغة متحيزة - الرأي / الحكم / الصورة النمطية
Neutral (Hedging) language = facts / accuracy / respect الدقة / الاحتزام / Gilda لغة محايدة

◆ النص القرائي | Reading: Habits for a Healthy Lifestyle

Read the first paragraph carefully. Underline the words that refer to th actions.

◆ تمرين | Exercise

Health experts regularly recommend that teenagers do at
least 60 minutes of moderate-to-hard physical activity every =

This doesn’t necessarily mean competitive sport. |
Activities such as cycling to school, swimming, or even a brisk
walk, all count towards the daily target. What makes exercise
particularly powerful is its effect on the brain: physical activity
causes the release of endorphins, chemicals that reduce
feelings of stress and anxiety at the same time as boosting
mood and self-confidence.

Furthermore, teenagers who exercise regularly tend to
sleep more deeply and report higher levels of concentration
during school hours. Despite these well-documented benefits,
a landmark WHO-led study found that more than 80% of
teenagers worldwide fail to meet the recommended daily
activity levels, largely due to sedentary screen-based habits.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

To what extent is physical activity important for reducing stress?
Support your answer with evidence from the text.

c. Nutrition, Sleep, and Recovery

A balanced diet is widely seen as the foundation of
physical and mental well-being. Nutritionists advise that
teenagers consume a varied range of fruits, vegetables,
whole grains, lean proteins, and healthy fats, while limiting |
their intake of processed foods, too much sugar, and high-
caffeine drinks. Poor nutrition has been directly linked to ©
fatigue, difficulty concentrating, and mood changes, all of
which affect academic performance. Sleep is equally
important, but it is often underestimated. Teenagers require
from eight to ten hours of sleep per night to support brain
development, emotional control, and physical recovery.
Research published in the journal Sleep Medicine found that —.
teenagers who slept fewer than seven hours per night were
more likely to report symptoms of anxiety and depression.
Setting up a regular bedtime routine, such as avoiding |
screens for one hour before sleeping, has been shown to
improve sleep quality.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

1. Is physical activity more important than diet for teenagers’ well-being?
Justify your position using evidence from the reading.

| 2. Sleep, nutrition, physical activity, and mindfulness: Which is the most important for

©) minatuiness, Social Connection, and Mindful Consumption

In recent years, mindfulness has moved from
specialist wellness programs into common| education.
Mindfulness involves paying deliberate attention to the
present moment, and can take many forms:
meditation, slow breathing exercises, yoga, or simply
sitting quietly without a device. Studies suggest that
even ten minutes of daily mindfulness practice leads
to measurable reductions in stress hormones over a
period of four to six weeks.

Alongside mindfulness, social connection plays a :
very important role in emotional health. Spending quality time with friends and family —
face to face rather than through screens — has been shown to reduce loneliness and build
the emotional resilience needed to cope with life’s pressures. Finally, many psychologists
advise us to be conscious not only of what we eat, but also of the media, products, and
information we absorb daily.

What it means to be of modern life:

Modern life moves faster than ever. Technology saves us time, yet we fill that time with

more tasks, screens, and pressure. How we choose to live - our daily habits, our diet, and
our sleep — shapes not only our physical health, but also our identity and sense of self.

living Well in a Complex World م

q The Case for Personal Responsibility

Many health professionals and philosophers argue that
‘| personal responsibility lies at the heart of a well-lived life. From
this perspective, every individual possesses the agency-the
‘| capacity to choose and act, and it is through the exercise of this
‘}agency that identity is formed. Choosing to exercise, to eat
‘| nutritiously, to sleep well, and to manage stress are not simply
‘| habits; they are expressions of who we are and what we value. *
:| Psychologists refer to this as having an “internal locus of control” -
‘|a belief that one’s own actions, rather than external forces,
‘| determine outcomes.

Research often supports the benefits of taking personal
!| ownership. A landmark study published in the Journal of Health :
: Psychology found that individuals who scored highly on self- wih: :
‘| efficacy measures-that is, those who believed in their ability to make positive changes-were
‘| Significantly more likely to maintain healthy behaviors over time, regardless of their
‘| socioeconomic background.

Furthermore, advocates of personal responsibility argue that framing health as someone
!| 01565 problem can be disempowering. When individuals attribute their choices entirely to
‘| outside forces, they risk becoming passive. Taking ownership, by contrast, builds confidence,
\resilience, and a sense of purpose, qualities that are central to a positive identity.

2 Social and Environmental Influences

However, the idea that individuals are entirely responsible for their
: | 01/0 health is increasingly challenged by social scientists and public
‘| health experts. Research in sociology demonstrates that where we grow
| up, how much money our family has, and the cultural rules we are raised
: with profoundly shape the choices available to us.

A teenager growing up in a neighborhood with no safe parks or
‘| affordable sports facilities cannot simply “choose” to exercise easily. A
‘| family with limited income may struggle to prioritize fresh vegetables
‘| over cheaper processed foods. These are not failures of willpower; they
‘| are structural barriers, obstacles built into the environment that restrict *
‘| genuine choice.

; Moreover, advertising and social media exert enormous pressure on
؛ | young people. Brands invest billions in campaigns designed to shape
‘|preferences and habits, often targeting teenagers who are at a
\particularly impressionable stage of development.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)
<ه To what extent is personal responsibility the most important factor in maintaining a|

healthy lifestyle? z
Justify your position using evidence from the reading. |:
°® To what extent can someone be “responsible” for choices they were never truly free to) |

Defend your opinion with evidence and explanation. 7

c. The Role of Government and Shared Responsibility

not an either-or choice; it does not belong entirely
to the individual or entirely to society, but is
shared. Governments, schools, families, and §&
communities all have a role in creating the
conditions that make healthy living possible and
accessible.

introduced public health policies intended to guide
and encourage better choices:

A third perspective argues that responsibility is

In recent decades, many governments have

taxes on high-sugar drinks, restrictions on junk

food advertising near schools, and national campaigns promoting physical activity.
Proponents argue that these measures do not remove individual freedom — they reshape the
environment so that the healthier choice becomes the easier choice.

Economist Richard Thaler’s concept of the “nudge” captures this idea precisely: small,

well-designed changes to the context in which decisions are made can shift behavior without
restricting freedom. From this viewpoint, responsibility is not abandoned by the individual — it
\is supported and shared by the structures around them.

Who is responsible for the way we live?

People have different opinions about who is responsible for a person’s lifestyle and well.)
being. Some believe that individuals should make their own healthy choices and take control of
their habits. Others argue that family, society, money, and social media strongly influence
people’s decisions. The article explores different perspectives on responsibility, well-being, and
the factors that shape human behavior and identity.

Find the word or phrase in the text that could meaningfully replace the words below:

Modern education aims to give students greater freedom to make choices about how they)
learn. When learners successfully complete difficult tasks, they often develop stronger
confidence in their own ability. However, some students still face a system-level obstacle,
such as limited internet access or high transport costs, that prevents equal participation.
Their belief about what controls outcomes can also affect how they respond to success
and failure. Students who develop the ability to recover from setbacks are usually better
prepared for future difficulties. Sometimes, a teacher may use a gentle prompt to
encourage better study habits without forcing anyone. In contrast, constantly making
decisions for learners can be making them feel less capable and less in control. Good
education should also encourage students to question unsupported ideas and make
learning materials easy to reach and use for everyone.

Cistening اذ Mental Heatth

A nationwide school health survey done in
Egypt in 2023 gathered data from 450
teenagers aged between 14 and 17, drawn
from both urban and rural schools across five
governorates. The study aimed to identify the
primary sources of stress among teenagers
and to examine the coping strategies they most
commonly adopted. Its findings a complex
picture of modern teenage life, in which
academic pressure, digital habits, and social
expectations interact in ways that are often
difficult to separate. The most significant 5
finding was that 68% of participants reported experiencing moderate to high levels of stress
during examination periods. When asked to identify the main causes, students ranked
academic pressure first, cited by 82% of respondents, followed by social media comparison
(61%), insufficient sleep (57%), and family expectations (49%). Clearly, the study found that
teenagers who did regular physical activity, defined as at least 30 minutes of exercise four or
more times per week, reported stress levels that were, on average, 34% lower than those of
their sedentary peers, even after controlling for academic performance variables. Cultural
background also was a Significant factor in how teenagers managed stress. Students from
families with strong collectivist values were considerably more likely to seek emotional support
from parents or siblings when facing difficulties. In contrast, students from more individualistic
household environments tended to rely on individual coping strategies, such as listening to
music, exercising alone, or spending time in nature. Neither approach was found to be
universally more effective. Rather, the study suggested that the availability and quality of
support networks were the decisive variables. The researchers also noted a significant gap in
access to school counseling services between urban and rural institutions. While 74% of urban
schools in the sample offered some form of counseling, the figure dropped to 31% in rural
schools. The study concluded that targeted mental health support programs in schools could

meaningfully reduce stress levels among teenagers.

Communities can differ greatly in the values they encourage. Some societies are)
strongly group-oriented, placing cooperation and shared goals above personal interests,
while others are more self-reliant, emphasizing independence and personal choice. These
differences may also appear between city-based communities and countryside areas,
where lifestyles and available services can vary considerably. Researchers should
remember that human behaviour is often changeable rather than fixed. For this reason,
some programs provide specifically directed support to particular groups. People also
need opportunities to communicate with one another so that they can exchange ideas and
experiences. Researchers may later classify the results according to importance or
\performance.

Several studies have examined the relationship between physical activity and stress)
levels among teenagers. Findings suggest that students who adopt a habit of exercising
regularly report significantly lower average stress levels than sedentary peers. This variation
remained even after researchers controlled for academic performance, showing that physical
activity itself plays a decisive role. Health institutions increasingly seek to promote exercise
\as a practical coping strategy.

© 1) Present Participial Phrases
JAD عبارات اسم

تتكون من V-ING « باقي العبارة وتستخدم في حالة المبني للمعلوم (Active) ولابد أن يكون الفاعل واحد في الجملتين
تستخدم بدلاً من الروابط ..../ When/ While /As /Once /After /As soon as /Because /By

* While he was walking to school, he met his friend.
= Walking to school, he met his friend.
* After he completed the project, he submitted it.
= Completing the project, he submitted it.
* Once (When) she finished her homework, she went to bed.
= Finishing her homework, she went to bed.
* Because he felt tired, he went to bed early.
= Feeling tired, he went to bed early.

وتستخدم بدلاً من ضمائر الوصل Who / Which / that

* The boy who is standing there is my brother.
= The boy standing there is my brother.

* The company which produces electric cars is expanding rapidly.
= The company producing electric cars is expanding rapidly.

© 2) Past Participial Phrases

تتكون من 0.0 + باقي العبارة و تستخدم في حالة المبني للمجهول (PASSIVE) ولابد أن يكون الفاعل واحد في الجملتين

When / As / Once / After / As soon as / Because /.... تستخدم بدلاً من الروابط

* When the building was damaged by the storm, it was closed.
= Damaged by the storm, the building was closed.

* Once the project was completed by the employees, it was presented to the manager.
= Completed by the employees, the project was presented to the manager.

* As soon as the room was cleaned by the servant, it was opened to visitors.
= Cleaned by the servant, the room was opened to visitors.

Who / Which / that من ضمائر الوصل Uy وتستخدم
* The student who was praised by the teacher won the competition.
= The student praised by the teacher won the competition.

*® The book which was written by Naguib Mahfouz is famous.
= The book written by Naguib Mahfouz is famous.

@)_—s33) Perfect Participial Phrases
' AD اسم الفاعل Le
ولابد أن يكون الفاعل واحد في الجملتين (ACTIVE) باقي العبارة في حالة المبني للمعلوم + Having+P.P تتكون من
When / As / Once / After / As soon as / Because /.... تستخدم بدلاً من الروابط

After she had finished the report, she sent it to her manager.

= Having finished the report, she sent it to her manager.

Because she had been warned about the danger, she was very careful.
= Having been warned about the danger, she was very careful.

تتكون من Having been+P.P + باقي العبارة في حالة المبني للمجهول (Passive) ولابد أن يكون الفاعل واحد في الجملتين

After the documents had been signed, they were sent to the office.
= Having been signed, the documents were sent to the office.
Because she had not been informed of the change, she arrived late.
= Not having been informed of the change, she arrived late.

© 4) Infinitive Phrases
عبارات الأصبار

تتكون من .101+ 10 + باقي العبارة وتكون غالبا بمعنى لكي او من اجل و ممكن تحل محل الفاعل أو محل المفعول
To improve his English, Ahmed reads books every day.
To save money, they decided to travel by train.

To protect the environment, people should reduce waste.
To get better results, students need to practice regularly.

للتعبير عن فعل متزامن being + P.P للحظ يمكننا أستخدام

Because it is used every day, the machine needs regular maintenance.
= Being used every day, the machine needs regular maintenance.

| 3( Read the text, then answer the questions:
a The Life We Show Others 7
For many young people, social media has become an important part of everyday life. They
use it to communicate with friends, follow celebrities, share photographs, and learn about
events around the world. However, the online world does not always show real life as it is.
People usually choose the best moments to share, while difficult or ordinary moments often
remain private.
This can affect the way young people see themselves. When they constantly compare ١
their lives, appearance, or achievements with carefully selected images online, they may
believe that other people are happier or more successful than they are. Such comparisons

siesta i لل ب ل

eee ءاطا”٠”٠©٠”ا6-ئ<])ا<ؤ<+”*>+<٠“5ج5ا5اج”<”ل”ااااداد<اائ  و با[طه«ص إ_إض

= Si Sagas

Modern lifestyles can also make this problem more complicated. Technology has made
many tasks faster and easier, but people often feel that they must always be available.
Messages, schoolwork, entertainment, and social media can fill much of the day. Without a
healthy balance, people may have less time for family conversations, outdoor activities, or
quiet moments.

For this reason, living well is not simply about having modern technology or being
successful. It also means making sensible choices about how we spend our time and how we
treat ourselves and others. Taking regular breaks from screens, developing real friendships,
and making time for enjoyable activities can help people maintain a healthier lifestyle.

Well-being, however, is not only an individual responsibility. Families and schools can help
young people understand how to use technology wisely and deal with pressure. Friends can
provide support when someone is experiencing difficulties. Communities can also create safe
environments where people feel comfortable discussing their problems without fear of being
judged.

Mental health is an important part of overall well-being, just as physical health is. A person
who looks happy online may still be facing serious difficulties in private. Therefore, people
should avoid making quick judgments about others based on what they see on social media.

Ultimately, living well in a complex world requires awareness, balance, and shared
responsibility. Technology can improve our lives when we control how we use it rather than
allowing it to control us. By supporting one another and making thoughtful choices,
_ individuals and communities can create healthier and more positive lives.

A responsible reader looks for an impartial account instead of accepting unfair reporting. )
Some online posts, however, can be prejudiced and strongly favor one viewpoint. Academic
writers may express complete certainty when evidence is strong rather than reducing the
force of their claims. Good reporting should present the complete picture, including both

themselves against other people. A writer may choose a descriptive adjective that simply
gives information and ask a direct question when a real answer is required. A softener can
reduce the strength of a statement. Constant criticism, however, may create self-doubt in

Screen Time and Young People (Research Report) >

Teenagers today spend an average of 7 hours a
day looking at screens, and that does not include
time spent on screens for school. Researchers and
parents alike are increasingly concerned about what
all this screen time is doing to developing minds.
One of the biggest concerns is the effect of social
media on teenagers’ self-esteem. Platforms like
Instagram and TikTok present carefully chosen
images of people's lives, making it easy for young
users to compare themselves negatively to others.
Studies suggest that heavy social media use is linked
to increased feelings of anxiety, loneliness, and
depression, particularly among teenagers. This
constant comparison can lead young people to develop a wrong sense of their own worth and
appearance. Experts note that the pressure to gain likes and followers can make teenagers
feel they are never good enough, which may cause them to withdraw from real-world social
activities.

Gaming: Passion or Addiction?

Video gaming is another form of screen time that sparks debate. Many experts point out
that gaming can develop problem-solving skills, hand-eye coordination, and teamwork.
However, when gaming becomes addictive—when a person cannot stop even when they want
to—it can interfere with sleep, schoolwork, and real-world relationships. The World Health
Organization officially recognized gaming disorder as a mental health condition in 2019.

The Benefits of Stepping Away

Research consistently shows that taking regular breaks
from screens can improve mood, concentration, and sleep
quality. Outdoor activities, face-to-face socializing, and
creative hobbies all provide benefits that screens simply
cannot replicate. Studies have found that even short periods
of time spent in nature or taking part in physical activity can
significantly reduce feelings of stress and restore mental

Finding Balance
The key message from researchers is not that screens are entirely harmful, as they are 80١
essential part of modern life, but that balance matters. Young people who set clear
boundaries around their screen use—such as avoiding phones an hour before sleep or

choosing screen-free times during meals—report feeling more in control of their habits and
less anxious overall. Mindful screen use, combined with regular physical activity and offline |

\social connections, gives young people the best chance of a healthy, fulfilling leisure life.

1) The Psychological Benefits of "Flow"

Ask anyone who has ever lost themselves in a painting, a _ ,
song, or a story, and they will tell you: creative activity is |
unlike anything else. Time slows down, worries fade, and
there is only the work in front of you. Psychologists call this
state “flow”: a condition of complete absorption in a
meaningful task. Research suggests that even thirty minutes
of creative engagement per day can measurably reduce
levels of the stress hormone cortisol, offering young people a
natural, accessible form of emotional recovery.

2) Creative Hobbies and Cognitive Growth

The benefits of creative leisure go far beyond simple
relaxation. Neuroscientists have found that making art,
whether drawing, playing an instrument, or writing, activates
multiple regions of the brain. It strengthens neural
connections and improves cognitive flexibility. Unlike passive
screen-based entertainment, creative activity demands that
the brain produce rather than merely consume. In other
words, creative hobbies literally make you smarter, and the
evidence for this is growing stronger with each decade of
research.

3) Self-Expression and Cultural Identity

For teenagers, creative arts offer something especially
valuable: a space for authentic self-expression. During the
teenage years, young people are constantly figuring out who
they are, who they want to be, and how they fit into the world.
Art provides a safe, non-judgmental arena for this
exploration. In Egypt, traditional art forms such as
calligraphy, arabesque design, music, and storytelling offer
additional layers of cultural meaning, connecting young
people to their heritage while allowing them to understand it
in anew and contemporary way.

4) Developing Valuable Collaborative Skills

Collaboration is another strong benefit. A school
orchestra, a drama production, a street art collective — all of
these require young people to listen to each other, and work
toward a shared goal.

These are skills that no classroom test can measure, yet
employers and universities consistently rank them among the
most valuable skills a young person can possess.

5) Building Resilience Through Failure

Perhaps most importantly, creative arts teach young people to embrace failure. In art,
there is no single correct answer. A painting that does not turn out as planned is not a failure ٠
— it is a lesson. A musician who plays a wrong note in a concert learns more from that :
moment than from any perfect rehearsal. Research by Arts Council England showed that I
young people who regularly engage in creative activities show greater emotional resilience
and are more likely to volunteer in their communities, suggesting that the benefits extend far
beyond the individual.

eee ee ee EEE EEE eee اي

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

1. To what extent do you think failure is important for learning? Give an example from the text.

( : Creative Arts as Leisure Activities

What it moonis'ts'§ie'@ Raa

Creative activities like drawing, making music, and writing play an important role in
young people’s lives. While many teenagers spend long hours on screens, the creative arts
offer a different and more meaningful experience. This text explores how creativity can
improve the brain function, emotional well-being, and personal development. It also shows
how creative activities help teenagers express themselves and build important life skills.

Read the text, then answer the questions:

When teenagers become fully absorbed in painting or music, they enter a state
psychologists call “flow.” This complete absorption allows worries to fade and helps the brain
recover from stress. Neuroscientists confirm that this kind of engagement produces real
emotional benefits rather than simple relaxation. Even a short, regular period of creative
activity can restore a young person's emotional well-being.

◆ فقرة التعبير عن الرأي | Opinion Paragraph

An opinion paragraph is a short piece of writing where you give your opinion about a
topic and support it with reasons and examples.

Conclusion:

The Importance of Learning a New Skill

| think that learning a new skill is a valuable way to improve our lives. It can
make us more confident and open new opportunities for the future.

This is because developing new skills helps people avoid wasting their free
time and encourages them to discover their abilities. They can also learn to
solve problems in different ways.

For example, learning a new language can help people enjoy
communicating with others and understand different cultures. Someone
may also try to learn coding, drawing, or cooking. At first, mistakes are
normal, but regular practice usually leads to improvement.

To sum up, I believe that developing new skills is worth the effort. People
should continue learning throughout their lives because every new skill can
bring useful knowledge, greater confidence, and new opportunities.

Pt e The Benefits of Sports Pyramid:
Cistening “> From Foundation to Peak
“Moving More: Why Sport Matters for Young People”*
, 88ل (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

_ &® To what extent are the mental and social benefits of sport more important than the ]
\. Physical ones? Explain your view.

The World Health Organization recommends that young people aged 5 to 17 should get at
least 60 minutes of medium to hard physical activity every day. Yet across the world, and
increasingly in Egypt, many teenagers fall far short of this target. A 2024 survey conducted by
the Egyptian Ministry of Youth and Sports found that fewer than 1 in 3 secondary school
students met the recommended weekly activity levels. The rise of inactive lifestyles, driven by
screen time and intense academic pressure, has created what some experts call a
“movement crisis" among the young.

The benefits of regular sport extend well beyond
physical fitness. Heart health, stronger bones, and healthy
weight management are the most obvious advantages, but
the psychological and social gains may be even more
significant. Psychologists have found that young people
who participate in team sports show higher levels of self-
esteem, better emotional regulation, and greater social
competence than their non-sporting peers. Sport teaches
young people how to lose gracefully, how to support
teammates in difficult moments, and how to persevere
through difficulty—all life skills of immeasurable value that
transfer directly into academic and professional contexts.

Access to sport is not the same for everyone. Some
young people have easy access to sports facilities, safe
places, and support from their families or schools.
However, others may not have these opportunities
because of cost, location, or lack of resources. This makes
it harder for them to take part in physical activity. It is
important to find ways to give all young people a fair
chance to enjoy sport.

The evidence on what makes young people stay active throughout their lives is
remarkably consistent. Enjoyment is the strongest predictor of long-term participation. When
young people are forced into competitive sport they dislike, or when failure is treated harshly,
they disengage. Young people who find sports they genuinely love—whether competitive or
enjoyable, individual or team-based—are far more likely to remain active into adulthood. The
challenge for educators and families is therefore to expand the range of sporting options
available, so that every student can find their own movement and carry it with them through

2 SRE (Justify - Reason - Evidence)
_ &® How far can sport help young people succeed in academic and professional life? I

\ Give reasons.

Sport is one of the most beneficial leisure activities for young people. It improves not only
physical health but also mental well-being. However, many teenagers today are becoming
less active due to screens and academic pressure. In this lesson, we will discover why
moving more can change your life.

Many experts describe modern inactivity as a movement crisis because people spend
too much time sitting and too little time exercising. What does a movement crisis mean
in this context?

a. A period when people compete in more sports.

b. A situation in which people do not get enough physical activity.

A problem caused by too much exercise. ع

d. A campaign encouraging professional athletes.

points than one another. What does competitive mean in this context?

a. Designed mainly for relaxation. b. Based on cooperation without winners
c. Involving people trying to win or perform better than others.

d. Intended only for individual practice.

they found a successful solution. What does persevere mean in this context?

a. To change the goal completely. b. To stop after the first difficulty.

c. To ask someone else to do the task.

d. To continue trying despite problems or difficulties.

Group activities can improve students’ social competence by teaching them how to
communicate effectively. What does social competence mean in this context?

a. The ability to work without speaking to others.

b. The ability to communicate and interact successfully with people.

The ability to remember information quickly. d. The desire to avoid social situations. ع
Long-distance runners need strong endurance to keep running for an extended period
without giving up. What does endurance mean in this context?

a. The ability to continue doing something difficult for a long time.

b. The skill of finishing every task immediately.

c. The habit of avoiding physically demanding activities.

d. The desire to compete with other people.

Constant stress can have psychological effects, influencing a person’s emotions,
thoughts, and mental well-being. What does psychological mean in this context?

a. Related mainly to physical strength. b. Connected only with financial conditions.
c. Related to the mind, emotions, and mental processes.

d. Concerned with environmental problems.

Read the text, then answer the questions:

Sport builds far more than physical fitness. Psychologists have found that young people
who participate in team sports develop stronger emotional regulation, higher self-esteem,
and greater social competence. Through sport, young people learn perseverance, how to
support teammates, and how to lose gracefully. These life skills, though immeasurable in
value, transfer directly into academic and professional success.

fitness?

A word family is a group of words that come from the same root, but have different forms
and functions in a sentence.

+ عائلة الكلمة هي مجموعة كلمات ترجع إلى نفس (root) Jo! ولكن تختلف فى الشكل والوظيفة داخل الجملة.
For example, look at the word act

Verb يخبرنا بما يفعله شخص ما أوشيءما.  فعل acl We must act quickly.

Noun يدل علي شخصاً أوشيئاً أوفكرة. اسم 0 We need immediate action.
fidjective Ase يصفااسما. active She is an active student.

fidverd Jie) bi Mle si يصف غالبًا فعنًا أوصفة actively She actively participates in class.

« ا Not Exactly a Relaxing Day

What it means to find meaning through unexpected outdoor iain

When we face unexpected situations outdoors, we often learn lessons that contribute to
our personal experience and mental growth. For instance, being caught in a sudden
rainstorm or navigating a difficult trail forces us to be fully present in the moment. This shift in
focus moves us away from daily stressors and towards the immediate environment.
Ultimately, overcoming these obstacles provides a sense of excitement and achievement that
makes the experience truly meaningful.

1. Read the extract carefully. As you read, | note the narrator’s changing mood and tone and
underline words or phrases that show how his feelings shift
My idea of leisure was simple: stay at home, watch a
series, and do absolutely nothing. I used to believe that the
best way to spend a free day was in complete stillness- a full
afternoon on the sofa, curtains half-closed against the
afternoon sun, and a cold glass of water on the table beside |
me. However, my friend insisted that! joinhim onacycling trip 3
along the Nile. “ It will be relaxing,” he said. “ You'll feel
amazing afterwards.” | believed him.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)
[co To what extent does the friend play an important role in the narrator’s experience? J

2. Read the next section. Give a sentence that summarizes the narrator's initial experience.

We started early in the morning, before the city had fully woken up. The air was still cool,
the road was quiet, and the Nile shimmered softly beside us. For the first hour, everything felt
exactly as promised. We cycled slowly, enjoying the view of the river, the sound of the water,
and the occasional call of birds settling on the bank. | even began to think that outdoor
activities were not as exhausting as I had always imagined and that my friend had been right
all along.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

c® Difficult experiences can lead to greater satisfaction. To what extent do you agree? 1
Give evidence from the text.

3. As you read the following sections, underline any word or phrase that shows the narrator’s
attitude changing.

اعد عند عد عند عد عد عند عد ع عد عد اع عد عد عا عد ع عد عن عد اع اغا عد عا اعد عد اع عا عا اله عا عا لا PS...

Then, things changed. The road became longer and less
shaded. The sun, which had been gentle and golden at dawn,
grew stronger and more direct as the morning wore on. My
energy began to disappear. My friend, who clearly had
considerably more experience, continued cycling as if nothing
had happened, as if the heat were simply decoration and the
distance were a minor detail. Meanwhile, ١ struggled to keep
up. My legs ached. My breathing was uneven. At one point, |
seriously considered giving up and calling a taxi home.

But ا er stopped me. Perhaps it was pride, the unwillingness to admit defeat in)
front of someone who was making it look so effortless. Or perhaps it was something less
comfortable to admit: I was genuinely curious whether ١ could finish. Step by step, ١ kept
going. We took short breaks in the shade, drank water, laughed at my complaints, and
continued moving forward. Each time | thought about stopping, ١ took one more pedal stroke
instead. When we finally stopped, ١ realized we had cycled much farther than ١ had expected. I
was exhausted in a way ١ had never quite experienced before - a deep, physical tiredness that
sat in my legs and shoulders like warm sand. But I was also strangely satisfied. Sitting beside
the Nile, drinking cold juice, watching the afternoon light change on the water, | felt a sense of
achievement that no quiet afternoon at home could ever have given me. That day changed my
understanding of leisure. It is not always about comfort or doing nothing. Sometimes, the best
moments come from effort, from pushing past the point where you wanted to stop, and from

\discovering — with some surprise — that you were more capable than you thought.

how you felt, and what you learned from the experience.

3) Read the text, then answer the questions:

6 More Than Just Free Time OY

When people hear the word “leisure”, they may think of watching television, using a
phone, or playing online games. Digital entertainment has certainly changed the way young
people spend their free time. With a few clicks, they can watch films, communicate with
friends, or play games without leaving home. These activities can be enjoyable, but spending
too much time in front of a screen may reduce the time available for other valuable
experiences.

Some young people have discovered that leisure can be more meaningful when it involves
creativity. Drawing, playing music, writing stories, photography, and making crafts allow
people to express their ideas and develop new skills. Unlike activities that simply provide
entertainment, creative hobbies often require concentration and active participation. They
may also help young people discover talents they did not know they had.

Physical activity is another important form of leisure. Sport can improve physical fitness,

but its benefits do not end there. Playing football with friends, swimming, cycling, or simply /

+ 5 الل ٠

2 walking can provide opportunities to meet people, reduce boredom, and develop confidence.
People who enjoy physical activities when they are young may also be more likely to continue
being active as they grow older.

However, finding the right leisure activity is not always easy. Students often have busy
schedules filled with lessons, homework, family responsibilities, and other commitments.
Some may believe that relaxing means doing nothing, while others may feel guilty when they
spend time on activities that are not connected with school.

In reality, useful leisure does not have to be completely relaxing. A difficult mountain walk,
for example, may be tiring, but it can also be exciting and memorable. Learning to play a
musical instrument may require patience, while joining a sports team may involve hard
training. Such experiences can still be valuable because they provide enjoyment while helping
people learn and grow.

The best use of leisure time therefore depends on balance. Digital activities have their
place, but they should not completely replace creative, social, or physical activities. When
people choose different activities and make room for meaningful experiences, free time
becomes more than a way to escape boredom. It becomes an opportunity to develop skills,

_ build relationships, and create memories that can last for years. زر

3) Read the text, then answer the questions:

‘Nadia used to enjoy taking photos, but recently, posting them on social media had
become stressful. Every morning, she checked the accounts of several popular teenagers
before getting ready for school. Their clothes always looked fashionable, their rooms seemed |
perfect, and their weekends were full of exciting activities. Nadia began to feel that her own |
life was ordinary and uninteresting.

One Friday, Nadia and her best friend, Jana, went to a small café after school. They |
laughed, talked about their week, and shared a piece of chocolate cake. Nadia took several —
photos, but before posting one, she spent nearly twenty minutes editing it. She changed the |
light, removed a small mark from her face, and carefully chose a picture that made the café I
look more expensive than it really was. Finally, she posted it with the words, “Perfect |
afternoon!”

A few minutes later, the photo received many likes. At first, Nadia felt pleased. Then Jana
looked at her and smiled. “It was a 7 afternoon,” she said, “but it wasn’t Pan We

oa 4 3 9) J م Consolidation Review (A)

waited thirty minutes for our food, and you almost dropped the cake!” -ٍٍ both a=
Suddenly, Nadia realized something important. She was doing exactly what other people did
online—showing only the best part of an experience.

That evening, Nadia looked again at some of the accounts she usually followed. This time,
she viewed them differently. Perhaps the people in those perfect photos also had difficult
days, worries, and ordinary moments that nobody saw. She understood that comparing her
complete life with someone else’s selected moments was unfair.

Over the next few weeks, Nadia changed some of her online habits. She unfollowed
accounts that regularly made her feel less confident and followed people who shared realistic
stories and positive messages. She also reduced the time she spent checking her phone.

Instead, she joined a school art club and began meeting Jana for walks on weekends.
Nadia did not stop using social media. She still enjoyed sharing photos and keeping in touch
with friends, but she no longer measured a good day by the number of likes she received. She
had learned that social media could be enjoyable and useful, but real confidence came from
accepting herself and appreciating the life she actually lived. =

The Stones That Remember: A Walk Through Karnak

When I stood at the entrance to Karnak Temple complex in
Luxor for the first time, I was not struck by its size although it is
truly enormous, but by its silence. In the early morning, before the
tour groups arrive, the Avenue of ram-headed Sphinxes stretches
southward in near-perfect stillness, each one carved from
sandstone that was already ancient when Cleopatra walked
beside it. In the space between the columns, with the first light
falling at a low angle across the hieroglyphics, it feels as if time
has not moved at all. Karnak is not a single building, but a
landscape of accumulated memory. Built over more than 2,000
years from the reign of Senusret in the Middle Kingdom to the
Ptolemaic era, the complex represents the longest continuous
building project in human history. Each Pharaoh added, changed,
or inscribed something, leaving layers of devotion and ambition
encoded in stone. The Hypostyle Hall alone contains 134 massive
columns, decorated from floor to ceiling. This space was designed
not simply to impress, but to communicate; it belonged to the gods, and the Pharaoh was s their
representative on earth.

What | find most significant, however, is not what has survived, but the question the site
invites us to ask as Egyptians today. Karnak was not built for tourists; it was built for a
civilization. Yet as we walk through its vast spaces, we are reminded that history is not only
something we observe, but something we interpret and carry forward. If a site of memory
belongs to everyone, how should it be understood, and whose voices should shape the way it
is told? These are not abstract questions; they are the questions that every Egyptian student
\Standing between those columns should feel encouraged to explore.

Since the Grand Egyptian Museum (GEM)
opened its doors to the public on 4 November 2025,
it has been described as the largest archaeological
museum in the world. Located near the famous
Pyramids of Giza, the museum covers 45,000 square
meters and houses more than 100,000 artifacts from
ancient Egypt. Among these treasures is the
complete collection of Tutankhamun, displayed
together for the first time in history. The impressive
Grand Staircase alone features 87 colossal royal
statues, arranged in chronological! order, offering
visitors a breathtaking introduction to thousands of

of the world’s oldest civilizations.

Before the age of civilization, humans lived through a long period known as prehistory.

The opposite opinion or idea against your point of view. *
<s الرأي أو الفكرة المخالفة لوجمة نظرك أو الحجة التي تعارض رأيك.
Good academic and journalistic writing acknowledges counterarguments before *
addressing them. This is done through concession clauses — they show the writer
is aware of other viewpoints.

◆ مهمة كتابة | Writing Task

Discursive Paragraph (Role - Audience - Purpose)

* Role: As a student contributor to your school’s heritage blog, write for a classmate
who thinks “museums are just for tourists”. Discuss the extent to which museums
play an important role in preserving history and identity.

Write a discursive paragraph of about (150-180) words.

1 Support

* Sentence starters: While some people use museums as..., they actually... because ...

* Stretch: Extend to a two-paragraph mini essay: one paragraph per perspective.

Topic Sentence:

First Perspective:

Museums play an important role in society, although people
have different views about their value in preserving history
and identity.

On the one hand, some people believe that museums are
mainly tourist attractions where visitors go to see ancient
objects and famous works of art. They may argue that history
can now be explored through books, websites, and virtual

tours without visiting a museum.

On the other hand, museums do much more than attract
tourists. They preserve valuable artifacts and provide real
evidence of how people lived in the past. While it is true that
digital resources make _ historical information easily
accessible, museums offer a direct connection with authentic
objects and cultural heritage.

From my perspective, museums are essential because they
help people understand their roots and strengthen their sense
of identity.

in conclusion, museums may serve tourists, but their greater
value lies in preserving history and passing cultural identity on
to future generations.

To “go viral” means to spread rapidly and widely online (often within 24 hours). This
spread is driven by emotional engagement (surprise, pride, or nostalgia) rather than historical
accuracy or truth.

( Inquiry Question

c® How can a historical event go viral? Give an example.
Discussion in groups of four:

c> Analyze: Have you ever seen a historical fact or image go viral on social media? Was
it accurate? How could you tell?

c® Evaluate: Why might people share historical content that is false or exaggerated?
What motivates this?

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

>< Should people trust viral historical posts just because they reach millions of people!
quickly? Justify your answer,

‘lancient Alexandria can be shared across

‘| qualified. A viral post may claim that the Great Pyramid was built in a single decade by a team

Why Viral History is Not Always True History 7

Never before in human history have historical
images, artifacts, and claims reached many people
so quickly. A single post about an Egyptian
pharaoh can accumulate millions of views within ,
twenty-four hours; a colorized photograph of

continents before a single historian has had the |
opportunity to verify its accuracy. The scale and
speed of this spread are, in themselves,
remarkable. The problem is that scale and speed
have no real relationship to truth.

The way social media platforms work increases this risk considerably. These platforms
are designed not to prioritize accuracy but to promote engagement. Research consistently
shows that engagement is driven by emotion. Content that causes surprise, pride, nostalgia,
or strong reaction spreads more rapidly than content that is measured, detailed, or carefully

of fifty engineers. Such posts often accumulate more shares than carefully sourced
corrections. In reality, the actual construction process unfolded over decades and involved
tens of thousands of workers.

The consequences for our understanding of history are significant. In Egypt, where the
relationship between ancient heritage and national identity is particularly important, the viral
spread of historical myths and false claims risks distorting the public’s understanding of who
they are and where they come from.

Rarely do virality and accuracy move in the same direction. Only through deliberate media
literacy — the developed habit of questioning sources, checking dates, and consulting multiple
perspectives — can citizens distinguish between the past that actually happened and the past

\that merely trends.

In countries like Egypt, where heritage is closely tied to national identity, false historica
claims carry particular risk. When people are unable to distinguish between verified facts and
viral trends, their understanding of their own heritage can become distorted. Media literacy,
therefore, is not just an academic skill but a way of protecting a shared historical identity.

Reading ‘ Media and Historical Events

To document a historical event means to create a lasting record of an occurrence through
primary evidence - such as a text, a photograph, or a video — that can be analyzed and
verified by future generations. Traditionally, this was the job of “official” gatekeepers like

\government ministries, academic historians, or professional news outlets.

What does it mean to document historical events? 1

( Inquiry Questions

1. What is ‘citizen journalism’?
2. How has social media changed the way historical events are documented?

Discussion in groups of four:

1. Explain: Have you ever witnessed a historical event documented on social media in real
time? What was your experience?

2. Evaluate: Is a smartphone video taken by a civilian more or less reliable than a news
broadcast? Justify your view.

, 88ل (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

c® Some argue that museums preserve artifacts for all humanity.
c® Others argue that they were taken without consent and belong to their original countries. |
1- To what extent should historical artifacts be returned to their countries of origin?
2- Who benefits from current museum displays - and who might lose out?
Think about both views before you decide on your answer.

For most of human history, news of a great archaeological discovery traveled slowly. A)

royal tomb found in the Valley of the Kings might be reported in a specialist journal months
after the excavation began, reaching only a small community of scholars and museum
professionals. The wider public, whether in Egypt or abroad, would wait years before seeing
even a photograph of what had been uncovered. Knowledge of the past was carefully
managed, moving through official channels before it reached ordinary people.

Social media has transformed this process in ..
remarkable ways. When an exciting find is made today,
whether a sealed tomb in Luxor, a submerged city off the
coast of Alexandria, or an ancient papyrus in the Delta, it
can be photographed, posted, and shared with millions of ;
people within hours. Official accounts run by
organizations such as the Ministry of Tourism and
Antiquities now use platforms like X and Instagram to
document discoveries in real time, allowing followers
from Cairo to Tokyo to watch history being uncovered.
For the first time, Egypt's extraordinary heritage is being
shared with the world at the very moment it is found.

There are challenges to consider, however. A post on X can be deleted, an Instagram story
expires after 24 hours, a TikTok video may be removed by its creator or by the platform's
moderation systems within minutes of going viral. Unlike a stone inscription or a printed
journal, social media documentation is fragile. There is also the question of accuracy. Not
every post about an Egyptian discovery is published by experts, and images or claims can
sometimes be misunderstood or shared out of context. Specialists argue that social media is

\most valuable when it works alongside careful academic documentation and preservation.

_ &®To what extent has social media transformed the speed at which historical |

Read the text, then answer the questions:

Museums like the Louvre and the British Museum display artifacts that trace the history of)
entire civilizations. However, critics argue that these displays often include a selective
version of history while excluding the perspectives of the countries the artifacts came from.
Preservation of these pieces is important, but so is verifying who truly benefits from their
exhibition. This debate raises questions about who has the right to document a culture's
\heritage.

1. What criticism do some people make about museum displays, according to the
passage?

2. Why does the passage suggest that preservation alone is not enough?

For centuries, historians relied on official gatekeepers to document important discoveries
before sharing them with the public. A royal tomb might be verified by scholars long before
ordinary people ever saw a photograph of it. This slow process ensured accuracy, but it also
excluded many voices from the conversation. Today, social media has transformed how
quickly such occurrences reach the wider public.

1. (Analyze) How did official gatekeepers traditionally document historical discoveries?

2. (Evaluate) Do you think this traditional process preserved accuracy better than today's
fast sharing?

News Report

A news report is a short piece of writing that informs people about a recent event.
مؤخرًا. gg أو Cyan Gay الناس p55 التقريرالإخباري هو قطعة كتابية قصيرة ©»

‘The opening sentence. It gives the most | 2

> € Tips for Writing a Good News Report

1. Write a clear and interesting headline. اكتب عنوانا واضحا ومثيرا للاهتمام
2. Start with a strong opening sentence (Lead). ابدأ بجملة افتتاحية قوية
3. Include the important information: What, Where and When. اذكر المعلومات المهمة: ماذا؟ أين؟ ومتى؟
4. Add important details about the event. أضف التفاصيل المهمة عن الحدث
5. Mention who discovered or was involved in the event. اذكر من اكتشف الحدتٌ أو شارك فيه
6. Explain why the event is important. الحدث مهم (DL وضّح
7. Put your ideas in a logical order. رتب أفكارك ترتيبًا منطقيًا
8. Use simple and clear language. استخدم لغة بسيطة وواضحة
9. Use the passive voice for formal reporting. استخدم المبني للمجهول في التقارير الرسمية
10. Finish with a short and relevant closing. اختم بخاتمة قصيرة وذات صلة

(Applied Example Aub مثال

Ancient Tomb Discovered in Luxor

A new ancient tomb was discovered in
Luxor, Egypt, this week by a team of Egyptian
archaeologists. The discovery was made during
an archaeological mission in the area. The tomb
contains several ancient objects, including
pottery, statues, and inscriptions on its walls.

According to the archaeologists, the tomb
is believed to belong to an important person
from ancient Egypt. The discovery is
considered important because it may provide
new information about the life and culture of
ancient Egyptians.

The news was shared with the public through the school magazine and local news
reports. Photos of the discovery were also shown to help people learn more about the site.

Experts say that more details will be revealed after the tomb is carefully studied. Further

investigations will also be carried out to discover more about the objects and the person
buried inside the tomb.

◆ 610 Prepositional Phrases of Time

غبازات الجر الدالة على الزمان

Prepositional phrases of TIME tell us when something happened or for how long.They are
essential for establishing sequence and duration in historical writing.

©@_Frepositional Phrases of Place
Prepositional phrases of PLACE tell us where something is or was, or where it happened.
They are essential in historical and descriptive writing.

- 3) Read the text, then answerthe questions: = = #### ©.
1 When the Past Speaks i °

A visit to a historical place can be very different from reading about the same place in a
book. Standing in an old building, walking through an ancient street, or seeing an object used
by people hundreds of years ago can make history feel more real. For this reason, historical
sites are often described as places of memory because they help communities remember
important parts of their past.

Museums also play an important role in preserving these memories. They collect objects, |
photographs, documents, and other forms of evidence and arrange them in exhibitions. |

OSSIAN

However, a museum does more than simply protect old objects. The way an exhibition is )
organized can influence the story visitors understand. Choosing which objects to display and I
explaining their importance can highlight some aspects of the past while giving less attention |
to others.
This does not necessarily mean that museums are inaccurate. Instead, it shows why ١
visitors should think carefully about historical information. A photograph, an object, or a |
written document can provide valuable evidence, but understanding its background is also |
important. Two people may even interpret the same historical event differently because they I
have different experiences or sources of information.
Digital technology has created new ways of preserving and sharing the past. Old |
photographs can be scanned and stored online, while historical documents can be made ©
available to people who live far from museums and archives. Social media has also allowed |
ordinary people to record important events as they happen. A photograph or video posted |
during an event may later become valuable evidence for historians.
At the same time, the speed at which information spreads online creates new challenges.
A historical photograph can go viral with an incorrect description, and thousands of people ٠
may share it before anyone checks the original source. Once a false explanation becomes
popular, it may be difficult to correct. :
For this reason, documenting history requires more than collecting information. People I
need to ask where information came from, compare different sources, and consider whether I
a claim is supported by evidence. Digital tools can help preserve the past, but they do not |
automatically make every piece of information reliable. i
The past belongs to everyone, but understanding it requires care. Historical sites,
museums, photographs, documents, and social media can all help future generations learn
about earlier times. When people use these sources thoughtfully, they can preserve |
memories without allowing inaccurate stories to replace historical evidence.

a Formal Report:

◆ النص القرائي | Reading: The Causes of Food Waste in Egyptian Homes

Do you think people waste more food today
than in the past?

Every year, a large amount of food is wasted around the
world. This is because people buy more than they need, store
food poorly, and misunderstand food labels. Food waste
increases in houses, supermarkets, and during special times and
festivals. However, simple actions such as better habits and

\awareness can help reduce this problem.

( inquiry Question
c® Where do you think most food waste happens: at home, at shops, or on farms? What
do you think is the biggest reason for food waste in Egypt?
Discussion in groups of four:

c® Explain: How does our perception of food safety influence the amount of food we
discard?

c® Analyze: To what extent does the modern supply chain — from supermarkets to
homes — contribute to the global food waste?

c® Evaluate: How can you, your family, and your school reduce waste?

( Introduction

This report looks at the main reasons why food is wasted in Egyptian homes. It uses
information from consumer surveys and local studies. The results show that food waste in
Egypt is a serious problem that needs to be addressed.

c® Project: What do you think the writer will say next about food waste?
(Findings (Causes)

In Egypt, one common cause of food waste is
buying more food than a family can use. During
Ramadan, some families may prepare or purchase
a large amount of food to welcome guests and
express generosity. Although generosity is a
positive and valued tradition, poor planning can
sometimes lead to more food being prepared than
needed. As a result, some of the extra food may be
wasted.

A second important cause is storing food
incorrectly. Many homes do not have good Dy.
refrigerators or dry storage. As a result, fresh food - especially bread, fruit, and vegetables -
goes bad faster than it should. Bread waste alone is a major issue in Egypt.

A third cause is confusion about dates on food packages. Many people throw away food
on or before its ‘best before’ date.

However, ‘best before’ only means that the taste or quality might change — it does not

تتم ا ااا ا ااا لزي انتما اا 0

mean the food is unsafe. The ‘use by’ date is the one that tells you when food is no longer safe

c® Discuss:
Which cause of food waste seems most common in your community?

7. ا

7 SRE (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

1. كا food waste more of an individual problem or a social problem?
Use evidence from the report to support your answer.
2. ا ا ee ee الابما الم tere ساي وبلا Me Seer بي ليمي

Egypt? Justify.

) Recommendations

Based on these findings, this report suggests three
actions.

First, schools should teach students about food shopping
and storage from an early age. Second, shops should be
required to give safe leftover food to food banks rather than
throw it away. Third, food labels should be made clearer so
that people understand the difference between ‘best before’
\and ‘use by’.

c® Evaluate:
Which recommendation would have the biggest impact? Why?

c® Conclusion:

The evidence shows that most food waste in Egypt can be reduced through education and better
habits. These changes do not cost a lot of money. If these recommendations are followed, the amount
of food waste in Egyptian homes could go down significantly within a few years.

Beating \_

Every day, teenagers are surrounded by brands, from the
moment they wake up until they go to sleep. These brands
are not just simple logos on products. They are carefully
designed to influence how people think and feel. Companies
use powerful ideas and emotions to connect their products to
identity and belonging. This raises an important question:
when we choose a brand, are we making our own decisions,

or are our choices being shaped for us?

( Inquiry Question
c® What is the difference between a product and a brand?

Discussion in groups:

Explain:

Which brands do you or your classmates like most? Why? <ه

Do you ever buy something because of the brand name? <ه
Analyze:

c® Why might two similar products have very different prices just because of the
Evaluate:

Do you think choosing a brand is more about your feelings or about the quality of <ه
the product?

More Than a Logo: How Brands Shape What We Buy* >)

Every day, by the time most teenagers in Egypt have
had breakfast, they have already made more brand choices ١
than they realize. The phone that wakes them up is one of
the most recognized devices in the world. The shoes by the
door carry a tick that is far more famous than the
company’s actual name.

Furthermore, the cereal box on the table features a
cartoon bird that has remained unchanged longer than
many brands have survived. None of this is an accident.

The biggest companies invest more heavily in brand-
building than in almost anything else. This is because a well-
known brand is more valuable than any single product. In addition, they spend more on

\emotional storytelling than on simply describing what their products do.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)
| c® Why do companies invest heavily in brand-building?

c® Use evidence. )
Find a word or phrase inthe paragraph that means; ----
1. put money and effort into something I ssa a lia cei
2. known by almost everyone Ee لحت تاج أ ee ae \
3. worth a lot/important ا اللا ERE RT عو RT Ree NUE MONS eRe وم اموق ;

A brand is much more than a logo. It is a set of
feelings and ideas, and the strongest brands build those
feelings more carefully and more consistently than
smaller companies ever could. When brand X puts its
tick on a shoe and writes “Just Do It”, it is selling not
only shoes, but also the idea that the person who wears
them is stronger, more determined, and more ready for
a challenge than someone who does not. Similarly,
when a phone brand presents a new phone in a clean
white room, it is selling the idea that its products are
simpler, more elegant, and more desirable than anything else on the market.

A branded product is almost always more expensive than a similar unbranded one. Even
though the quality difference is often smaller than people expect, they still choose the brand
\because the feeling it gives them feels worth far more than the price difference.

, SRE (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

| °® To what extent do strong brands affect people more than smaller companies ? ]
| ©® Support your answer with evidence.

و .

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

To what extent do brands sell feelings rather than products? <ه
c® Use evidence from the text to support your answer. |
you are crea’ anew brand for ers. ©
What product does your brand sell? 17 .4

سسب سما لاا المسسم سا الام ا تاك 66115676061 Virite your. 06611716 shorts lel CRG 2

Psychologists who study how people make choices have found something interesting.
When people see the logo of their favorite brand, the part of the brain linked to personal
identity becomes more active than when they see an unknown label.

As a result, choosing a brand is rarely a simple or purely practical decision. This means
that when someone chooses X over Z, they are often making a deeper choice than simply
picking a more comfortable shoe-they are saying something about who they are, or who they
most want to be.

Understanding why we prefer one brand to another can be an important step towards
mindful consumption. For instance, for many young people in Egypt, wearing the most
popular brand feels more important than wearing the most practical one, because brands
have become one of the most powerful languages we use to show where we belong.
\ Admittedly, not everyone is equally influenced by brand image.

/- SRE (Justify - Reason - Evidence)
__ 1. To what extent do brands influence our choices? Use evidence from the text.
2. Why do you think the work of brands is important for consumers?

Although companies benefit greatly from this power, the influence of brands also raises
one of the most important questions in modern life: are we truly free when we make choices,
or do companies create our preference for us? On the other hand, understanding how brands
work gives us a real advantage as consumers. This helps us avoid blind brand loyalty, where
we buy a product based on emotion rather than quality. The most successful brands do not
just sell products. They sell the feeling of belonging somewhere.

Consequently, this feeling becomes stronger, and far more personal. It also becomes
harder to resist than any simple advertisement could ever be. Overall, the more aware we are
of how brands shape our thinking, the better and freer our choices can become.

c® “The more aware we are of how brands shape our thinking, the better and freer
ل our choices can become.” Do you agree?

buying decisions? Why or why not? Use ideas from the text to support your answer.

An opinion article is a piece of writing in which the writer expresses their personal
views, beliefs, or opinions about a particular topic and supports them with reasons,
examples, and evidence.

© مقال الرأي هو نوع من الكتابة pies فيه الكاتب عن oy Bi Gloag أو معتقداته أو آرائه الشخصية حول موضوع معين.
ويدعمها بالأسباب والأمثلة والأدلة.

When you write an opinion article, turn your insight into an intervention. .

Don’t just observe the world, but use your analysis to pinpoint a specific problem. .

Tips to Write a good Opinion article

1. Write a short, catchy title that shows your opinion. اكتب عنوانًا قصبرا وجذابا يعبر عن رايك

2. State your main opinion clearly in the introduction. اذكر رايك الرئيسي بوضوع في المقدمة

3. Support your opinion with an example or evidence. ادعم رأيك بمثال أو دليل

4. Give a second reason and support it with an example. وادعمه بمثال Lil اذكر سببا

5. Mention one point from the other side. اذكر نقعلة واحدة من وجهة النظر الأخرى
«6. Restate your opinion in the conclusion. أعد التاكيد على رايك في الطائمة |

a How apps use your time
Many teenagers use social media every day without thinking about how these apps َي
work. Although these platforms seem free, they actually collect information about users’
behavior and interests. Apps are carefully designed to keep people online longer through)
features that make it hard to stop scrolling.

While social media has benefits, it is important for users to understand how their time and
\attention are being used.

C Inquiry Question

©® How much time do you spend on your phone each day? What do you use it for most?

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

°® Do you think you control your phone - or your phone controls you?
Give reasons.

c® Explain
٠ What apps do you spend the most time on?

c® Analyze

c® Evaluate
* Is using a phone or an app similar to buying a product? Do you agree or disagree?

1. You have probably heard this phrase before:

“If you are not paying for the product, you
are the product." Most people use social
media apps for free. But nothing is ever truly
free. Every minute you spend scrolling, the
app is collecting data about you—what you
like, what you ignore, how long you stop on
each image, and what makes you react. This
data is then sold to companies who want to
show you advertisements. Your time and
attention are not free; they are being bought and sold.

2. Apps are designed to keep you online for as long as possible. Features like infinite scroll

where the feed never ends, and autoplay where the next video starts automatically,
were not created by accident. They were built by teams of designers and
professionals whose job was to make you stay on the app. By the age of 17, the
average teenager has already spent more time on social media than in all their years of
school.

. The effects on concentration are also serious. Research has found that simply
receiving a notification, even if you do not open it, can make it harder to focus on a
task. Your brain is waiting for the reward of checking the message, and this waiting
takes up mental energy. Students who keep their phones nearby while studying
perform less well than those who put their phones in another room.

. None of this means that apps and social media are completely bad. They help people
stay connected, access information, and be creative. However, it is important to
understand how these platforms work and who benefits from your time online. The first
step to being in control of your digital habits is simply knowing that your attention has a
value. Companies have always known this; now it is time for users to know it too.

a. It is deleted after 24 hours. b. It is sold to advertising companies.

c. It is shared with other users. d. It is kept private.

a. Notifications and alerts b. Infinite scroll and autoplay

c. Videos and images d. Messages and comments

a. They help students focus better.

b. They only affect concentration if you read them.

c. They make it harder to concentrate even if not opened.

d. They have no effect on concentration.

What is the speaker’s main message about apps and social media?
a. They should be banned for teenagers.

b. They are completely harmful and should be avoided.

They have benefits but users should understand how they work. ع

d. They are the most important part of modern life
