Structure of a Review _©

c® A well-written review follows a clear four-part structure.

c® Each paragraph has a specific purpose. لكل فقرة هدف محدد
Paragraph 1 - Introduction المقدمة
c® Name the subject of the review. اذكر الموضوع الذي تقوم بمراجعته
c® Give essential background information. قدّم المعلومات الأساسية والمهمة عنه.
<ه State your overall impression in one sentence اذكر انطباعك العام عنه في جملة واحدة.
Paragraph 2 ~ Positives الايجابيات
<ه Describe the strengths of the subject. اذكر نقاط القوة في الشيء الذي تراجعه
c® Use specific examples or evidence. Bal 9) استخدم أمثلة محددة
<ه Explain why each point is positive. وضّح ماذا تعد كل نقطة إيجابية
Paragraph 3 - Negatives السلبيات
c® Discuss the weaknesses or limitations fairly. ناقش نقاط الضعف أو أوجه القصوربشكل عادل
c® Avoid being overly tough. تجنب أن تكون قاسيًا أو ناقدا بشكل مبالغ فيه
c® Use a balanced, simple and professional tone. استخدم أسلوبًا متوازنا وبسيطًا ورسميًا
Paragraph 4 - Conclusion الخاتمة
c® Summarize your overall judgement تقييمك أو حكمك العام. nab)
c® State who you would recommend it to, and why اذكر لمن توصي به. ووضّح السبب.
<ه End with a memorable final sentence _ senate ats المتتم

The Psychology of the Sale:
Why We Buy What We Do Not Need

Sale events make people feel excited and believe they are
saving money! However, companies use clever tricks to
influence our decisions and push us to buy more. These tricks
create urgency and make products seem more valuable than
they really are. In the end, many people spend more than they

( Inquiry Question

c® In the Egyptian market, we see both terms: “Black Friday” and “White Friday”. Why
do you think some brands choose one over the other? How does the name influence
your decision?

°® Have you or your family ever bought something you did not need because it was on

c® What problems can big sale events like Black Friday cause for people and the

<ه Do sales events really help people save money?

Justify your answer with reasons and evidence from the text. ee,
©@ —_ the Tricks Behind Sale Events
The word for” in the text is closest in to:
a. avoiding b. being tricked by c.  understanding d. comparing

set of very clever tricks?

Every year, millions of people around the world wait for events
like Black Friday to go shopping. They feel excited as they plan
their purchases in advance and believe they are making smart

choices. But are they really saving money, or are they falling for a

j ce

What happens to the jacket price in the example?

One of the most common tricks is called loss aversion. Imagine a jacket that costs 400)
Egyptian pounds. You do not really need it. Then a sign appears: ‘Was 800 LE, Now 400 LE,
Save 50%!’ Suddenly, the jacket feels much more
attractive. Your brain tells you, ‘If I don’t buy it, ١
will lose the chance to save 400 pounds.’ Of
course, you have not lost anything, but that is
how our brains work. Not buying something feels
like a loss.

Name two tricks used in sales.

Sale events also use other tricks. Phrases like
‘Only 3 left in stock!’ create a feeling of urgency.
You feel that you must decide immediately or you
will miss the chance. When you see many other ™ —
people buying something, you think it must be a good ee, And أ countdown clocks on
websites force you to decide quickly, before you have time to think carefully. All three tricks
work together to push you towards buying.

Do you think people really save money during sales? Why or why not?

Research has found something surprising: people who shop during sales actually spend
more money overall than people who do not. They feel they have saved money, but in fact they
have spent more. The sale is not designed to save you money. It is designed to make you

\Spend more and feel happy about it.

Find the word or phrase in the text that could meaningfully replace the words below:

Many online stores now hold a similar sales event at a different time of year, sometimes in)
summer, offering discounts almost as big as the famous winter sale. Shoppers often feel a
strong fear of missing out on a good deal, worrying that if they wait, the price will rise again
the very next day. Small timers on shopping websites, showing minutes and seconds ticking
away, are placed next to popular items to add pressure to act fast. This is a common strategy
used by online retailers to turn browsing into a quick order before the customer changes their
mind. Not every deal is as limited as it appears, so take a moment before you buy something
the site describes as almost sold out.

Read the text, then answer the questions:

In Egypt, some brands use the term “White Friday” instead of “Black Friday” to attract
more customers during the winter season. Companies believe that a positive-sounding name
can excite shoppers and encourage them to visit stores. Marketing teams carefully decide
which name will create a stronger emotional reaction. For many customers, the name itself
\becomes part of the shopping experience, even before they see any actual prices.

Friday"?

2. Evaluate: Do you think the name of a sale event really changes how much people buy?

The Choice Behind the Purchase ae ل

Walking through a supermarket, customers face hundreds of choices. Different brands
offer similar products, while colourful packages and attractive advertisements compete for
attention. Sometimes people choose a product because it is familiar, but a well-known brand I
does not always mean that a product is the best choice. Price, quality, ingredients, and the
way a product is produced may also be important.

Food provides a clear example of how everyday choices can have wider consequences.
Families sometimes buy more food than they need because they are attracted by large
packages or special offers. When some of this food is not eaten, it may eventually be thrown
away. The money spent on it is lost, but food waste also means that the water, energy,
transport, and work used to produce the food have been wasted.

Online shopping has added another level to consumer choice. People can now buy
products at any time without visiting a shop. Websites and applications remember what users
have searched for and may suggest similar products. This can make shopping easier, but it
can also encourage people to buy things they had not planned to purchase.

Advertising can make this influence even stronger. A product may be presented as a way
م become more successful, attractive, or popular rather than simply as something useful. )

(Discounts can also create a feeling that an opportunity will disappear if the customer does not )
act immediately.

White Friday and similar sales events are good examples of this sales culture. Some
discounts can genuinely help customers save money, especially when they have already ١
planned to buy a necessary product. However, a low price does not automatically make a
purchase sensible. Buying an unnecessary product for less money is still spending money
unnecessarily.

Conscious consumers therefore need to pause before making a purchase. They can ask
whether they really need the product, compare prices and quality, and consider whether the
advertisement is influencing their decision. They can also plan food purchases carefully to
reduce waste.

Having many choices can be useful, but choice only becomes powerful when people use it
thoughtfully. A conscious consumer does not reject shopping or advertising completely.
Instead, they learn to look beyond the brand, the discount, or the attractive message and

make decisions based on real needs and reliable information.

3) Read the text, then answer the questions:

/ When Places Remember: x
A historical landscape is more than a collection of old buildings, monuments, and ruins. It
is a place where events from the past have left visible or invisible traces. A battlefield, an
ancient street, a former factory, or even an ordinary house can become a site of memory ٠
when people connect it with important events, experiences, or individuals. Such places help
communities remember their past and consider how it continues to influence the present.
However, sites of memory do not tell their stories automatically. The meaning of a place
often depends on how it is preserved and presented. A ruined building, for example, may |
appear meaningless to a visitor who knows nothing about its history. oS

2 Signs, photographs, recorded memories, and personal objects can provide context and
transform a visit to an unfamiliar place into a powerful historical experience. In this way, |
interpretation becomes as important as preservation.
This means that museums act as storytellers, but storytelling also involves responsibility. |
Museums must decide whose experiences to include and which voices may have been ١
ignored in the past. Traditional exhibitions often focused on powerful leaders and major |
political events. Today, many museums also include the experiences of workers, women, |
children, minority communities, and ordinary families. By presenting different perspectives, |
museums can offer a more complex understanding of history.
Technology has also changed how historical stories are communicated. Interactive |
screens, digital reconstructions, audio recordings, and virtual reality can help visitors
imagine places that no longer exist or events that no longer occur. Nevertheless, technology I
should support the historical story rather than distract from it. Impressive digital effects are of |
little value if visitors leave without understanding why a place or object matters. ا
Ultimately, historical landscapes and museums connect memory with the present. They |
remind us that history is not simply a list of dates and events. It consists of human experiences |
that can be remembered, interpreted, and sometimes debated. By reserving significant
places and telling their stories carefully, societies can help future generations understand |
both where they came from and how the past continues to shape their world. ل

‘ A. Mass Media *

◆ النص القرائي | Reading: The Climate Crisis: Understanding Our
Read the first paragraph carefully. Guess the me of the words in bold.

The Earth’s climate is changing at a never-before-seen rate.
Scientists around the world agree that human activity is the
primary driver of what we now call the climate crisis. Since the
industrial Revolution, the burning of fossil fuels - coal, oil, and
natural gas - has released billions of tons of carbon dioxide (CO2)
into the atmosphere. As a consequence, these emissions create
what is known as the “Greenhouse Effect”, which traps heat and
\raises global temperatures.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

To what extent do you agree that humans are mainly responsible for climate change? <ه
Support your answer with reasons and evidence from the text. _

The effects are already being felt across the planet. In the
Arctic, ice sheets are melting at an alarming rate. Glaciers that ,
have existed for thousands of years are getting smaller. As a result
of this rapid melting, sea levels are rising, threatening coastal
communities and low-lying island nations. Scientists predict that
without immediate action, sea levels could rise by up to one meter
لاط 2100, making hundreds of millions of people homeless.

◆ JRE | (Justify - Reason - Evidence)

How do you think climate change is an urgent global crisis? <ه
Give reasons and evidence to support your claim.

2. Extreme weather events

Extreme weather events are becoming more frequent and
more severe. Because of rising global temperatures, hurricanes,
wildfires, droughts, and floods are occurring with greater
intensity. In 2023 alone, wildfires damaged parts of Canada,
Greece, and Australia. Record-breaking temperatures were
recorded on every continent. Consequently, the human and

\economic cost of these disasters is shocking and continues to

3. Biodiversity loss

Biodiversity is also under serious threat. Since habitats shift
and temperatures change, countless species struggle to adapt.

Coral reefs, often called the rain forests of the sea, are dying
due to ocean warming and acidification. As a result, the Great
Barrier Reef has experienced mass bleaching events repeatedly in
recent years. Experts warn that human activity has led to the sixth
‘samen extinction event in Earth’s history.

4. Food security threats

Agriculture and food security are at risk too. Due to changing
rainfall patterns and extreme heat, crop production is being
reduced in many parts of the world. As a result, regions that were
once fertile are becoming increasingly dry. Meanwhile, other
areas face disastrous floods that destroy harvests. Sadly, the
poorest communities, who contribute the least to climate change,
\are often the hardest hit.

5. The urgency of action

The scientific consensus is clear. If global temperatures rise
by more than 1.5°C above pre-industrial levels, the results could
be disastrous and irreversible. The window for action is
narrowing. Every fraction of a degree matters. The decisions we
make today, individually and collectively, will determine the kind of
\planet we leave for future generations.

Scientists warn that the long-term shift in the world's weather patterns is affecting nearly)

\food supplies for millions of people.

Dr. Layla Hussein is talking to Marcus Green about the global renewable energy
transition.

Host: Welcome back to Green Futures. I'm joined today by Dr. Leila Hussein, energy
economist and clean energy supporter. Today, we're focusing mainly on the
success stories and the potential of the energy transition, really celebrating
what's working. Dr. Hussein, let's start with the basics. Which renewable
energy source is leading the charge right now?

Dr. Leila Hussein: Solar, without question. Solar energy is currently the fastest-growing
renewable source worldwide, and the cost of solar panels has fallen by nearly
90% in the last decade. That means that in many countries, renewable energy is
now actually cheaper than fossil fuels. Wind and solar together now provide a
significant share of global electricity, and that share is growing every year.

Host: So, what are the real-world success stories you find most encouraging?

Dr. Leila Hussein: There are many. Take Denmark, a country that has successfully
transitioned to generating over 60% of its electricity from wind. And
importantly, this transition has created thousands of new jobs in engineering,
manufacturing, and maintenance. The idea that moving to renewables means
losing employment is simply not true; in fact, the opposite is happening.
Developing nations are also increasingly leapfrogging fossil fuels entirely and
moving straight to renewables, which is extraordinary. Green hydrogen is also
emerging as a promising solution for heavy industry where direct electrification
is difficult.

Host: What about the challenges? Is battery technology still holding things back?

Dr. Leila Hussein: Battery technology was a major obstacle, but not anymore. Advances in
battery storage are rapidly solving the problem of intermittency—the fact that
the sun and wind are not constant. Storage is the real game-changer here. I
should also add that this transition requires both public and private investment
working together; public investment alone is simply not enough. We need
businesses, governments, and international institutions all committed. And on
that note, | must be honest: not all governments have committed to a fully
renewable future yet. That remains one of our biggest political challenges.

Host: So where does that leave us overall?

Dr. Leila Hussein: I am genuinely optimistic. The move away from fossil fuels is no longer
just an environmental choice; it is an economic one. With the right investment
and support from governments and businesses, a fully renewable energy grid is
absolutely achievable within our lifetime. In our next segment, we will explore
the policy frameworks that are making this possible.

One challenge with wind and solar power is that they don't produce electricity all the time, )

politicians worry about the financial cost of replacing old power plants too quickly, fearing it
could raise electricity prices in the short term. Most energy experts agree that a fully clean
power grid is a realistic goal within the coming decades, especially as battery technology

Read the text, then answer the questions:

Battery storage is often called a game-changer in the renewable energy transition
because it solves the problem of intermittency—the fact that sunlight and wind are not always
available. When excess energy is stored efficiently, it can be released whenever demand
rises, regardless of weather conditions. This achievement is helping many countries move
closer to a fully sustainable power grid.

الملهارات

An informational leaflet is a short piece of writing that gives the reader clear and useful
information about a topic in an attractive and easy-to-read way.

عنوان جذاب q Eye-catching Headline تَِ
Start with a short and interesting headline that immediately
catches the reader's attention.

You can use:
A question: © Ex. Want to Save Energy and Protect the Planet?
A statement: c® Ex. Clean Energy, Better Future!

العناوين الفرعية Subheadings

Divide your leaflet into short sections, and give each section a
clear subheading. م النشرة إلى أجزاء قصيرة. وضع عنوانًا فرعيًا واضحًا لكل جزء. “md
c® Ex. Why Choose Renewable Energy?
c® Ex. What Can You Do? c® Ex. Benefits for Our Planet.

> © Bullet Points النقاط المختصرة
Use bullet points to present important information quickly and
clearly. استخدم النقاط لعرض المعلومات المممة بشكل مختصر وواضح وسهل القراءة.
c® Ex. Renewable energy can:

-reduce carbon emissions. - provide cleaner energy.
- protect the environment.  - create new jobs.
> © تتم to Action دعوة القارئ لاتخاذ إجراء

At the end, encourage the reader to take a specific action.
في النهاية. شجع القارئ على القيام بتصرف معين.

Use strong action verbs:

c® Choose cleanenergy! © Save energy today!

Switch to solar today! © Start making a difference now! <ه

2 G rriendty Tone and Register أسلوب ودود ومناسب للقارئ
Use language that is friendly, simple, and informative,
especially when writing for young people.

استخدم لغة ودية وبسيطة ومفيدة. Gols ]15 كانت النشرة موجمة للشباب.

instead of complex language, use straightforward sentences such as:
من لغة معقدة. استخدم جملا" مباشرة مثل: “Vay

You can help protect our planet. <ه

c® Together, we can make a difference.

- 6 Visuals الصور والرسوم
Use suitable pictures, icons, charts, or diagrams to support
the information.

استخدم الصور gi الأيقونات gi الرسوم البيانية أو المخططات المناسبة لدعم المعلومات.
For example, a leaflet about renewable energy could include:
solar panels — wind turbines — simple energy diagrams <<

What is Egypt’s plan to turn green?

Egypt is taking significant steps toward a greener future. In this lesson, you will read |:
‘| about real environmental initiatives across the country, evaluate their effectiveness, and
! consider what more can be done—by governments and by individuals.

inquiry Question

Egypt’s environment?

c® Evaluate: Which is more important for Egypt’s green future, government projects or I
individual actions? ‘

To what extent does the Benban Solar Park help protect the environment ? هه

In recent years, Egypt has taken important steps to protect the environment and move):
: towards a greener future. One of the most well-known projects is the Benban Solar Park,
‘ which is one of the largest solar energy projects in the world. It produces clean electricity
١ using sunlight and helps reduce pollution caused by fossil fuels. Another major initiative is the
أ Go Green initiative, launched by the Ministry of Environment. This project aims to raise:
‘ awareness among citizens about protecting natural resources, reducing plastic use, and):
: recycling waste. It encourages people, especially young learners, to take responsibility for :
their environment. Egypt is also working on improving cities through the Green Cities):
‘| program, which includes projects in Cairo such as waste recycling, clean transportation, and |:
:| energy efficiency in buildings. ,

These projects aim to make cities
‘ healthier and more sustainable places to
‘live. In addition, the government and

1 recycling projects, especially in electronic

١ Manage e-waste safely and encourage

1 their daily habits and oo green solutions. :

Find the word or phrase in the text that could meaningfully replace the words below:

For over a century, most countries have relied on coal, oil, and gas to produce electricity)
and power vehicles. In recent years, however, power that comes from natural sources like the
sun and wind has grown rapidly as costs have fallen. Building new solar and wind farms
requires large amounts of money put into equipment and infrastructure before any electricity
is produced. Governments argue about whether ambitious climate targets are related to
money and industry realities, since some plans could affect jobs in traditional energy sectors.
Many experts still believe these goals are possible to reach within a few decades if investment
continues at the current pace. In volcanic regions, some countries also produce electricity
using energy taken from heat deep inside the Earth, which provides power both day and

action, analyze arguments and evidence, and plan your own Eco-Leaders program.

Young Egyptians Taking Action for Our Planet *

c® “An original pedagogical text custom-authored by the curriculum development أ
team to highlight local grassroots youth activism and engineering innovations, |:
engineered specifically to serve as a baseline model for reporting verbs and):
reported speech transformations.”

Last spring, the Egyptian Youth Environment
‘| Network held its annual conference in Cairo.
‘| The conference coordinator, Ms. Hana Karim,
: opened the event by saying that she had never
: 8680 so much determination from young
‘|Egyptians in all her years of environmental
:|work. She added that the solutions to Egypt’s
‘environmental challenges were already being
‘|developed by students and young innovators
١| across the country.

One inspiring story came from Omar Taha,
‘!an engineering student from Cairo University.
:| He told the audience that his team had designed
‘| a low-cost solar water heater that could be installed on ordinary rooftops. He explained that):
‘| the device could reduce a family’s electricity bill by up to forty percent. When a journalist ١
‘|asked him whether the technology was affordable for lower-income families, Omar replied |:
: ae the total cost of materials was less than three thousand Egyptian pounds.

Another memorable moment came from Farida Mustafa, a sixteen-year-old from Luxor.)
She said that she had started a Nile clean-up campaign with just five friends and that it had
grown to over two hundred volunteers within a year. She warned that the river was changing
rapidly and that their generation had a responsibility to protect it. She encouraged everyone
to take action, however small, in their local community.

As the conference closed, Ms. Karim reminded the delegates that Egypt was specially
positioned to lead environmental innovation in Africa. She told the young participants that the
decisions their generation made in the coming years would shape the future of the country

and the planet.

Despite several failed attempts, Salma showed great determination and continued
working until she solved the problem. What does determination mean in this context?
a. A strong will to finish a task. b. A fear of making difficult decisions.

irrigation. What does innovator mean in this context?

a. A person who studies old inventions.

b. A person who creates new ideas or solutions.

c. A person who sells expensive equipment.

d. A person who follows traditional methods.

c. A player avoids competitions because they seem difficult.

d. A learner changes goals whenever a task becomes challenging.

Find the word or phrase in the text that could meaningfully replace the words below:

Not all environmental news is bad. In a few protected areas, damage to local wildlife has)
actually slowed to a reassuring, gentle pace, giving scientists some hope. Some storms this
season have been unusually mild, causing far less destruction than experts had originally
predicted. However, there remains real disagreement among certain groups about how much
of the recent change is caused by human activity, with a small number of scientists still
questioning the data. Sadly, land that was once barren and impossible to farm rarely becomes
rich again without years of careful work. Meanwhile, coastal engineers note that in a handful
of specific bays, unusual local currents have actually caused sea levels to drop slightly,
though this remains extremely rare. In truth, only a small part of the scientific community
\disagrees, while the vast majority accept the overall picture painted by the evidence.

Read the text, then answer the questions:

Many young Egyptians are becoming innovators in the fight against climate change. At a
recent summit, a group of engineering students presented a low-cost device that uses solar
energy to reduce electricity bills for ordinary families. Their innovation was praised for being
affordable, even for lower-income households. The audience was inspired by their
determination to solve real problems.

1. What makes the students’ invention especially useful for families?

2. How did the audience react to the students’ presentation?

At an annual conference organized by an environmental network, several delegates
discussed how young innovators are addressing Egypt's environmental challenges. One
journalist asked whether ordinary citizens could really make a difference. A delegate replied
that even small actions, when repeated by many people, create large-scale change and
reduce the country's environmental problems.

1. What question did the journalist ask the delegate?

2. According to the delegate, how can small actions lead to large-scale change?

TOTTI ١١ Adverbials of Frequency and Degree and Manner

An adverbial is a word or phrase used to express frequency, time, place, purpose,
degree, or manner.

3) Read the text, then answer the questions:

A Greener Future Starts Today 2

For many years, scientists have warned that human activities are changing the climate.
Burning large amounts of fossil fuels releases greenhouse gases into the atmosphere. These
gases trap heat and contribute to global warming, which can affect weather patterns,
ecosystems, agriculture, and communities around the world.

The effects of climate change are not limited to one country. Rising temperatures,
changing rainfall patterns, and more frequent extreme weather events can affect food
production and water supplies. Because these challenges cross national borders, solving
them requires cooperation between governments, businesses, scientists, and communities.

One important part of the solution is the development of renewable energy. Solar and
wind power can produce electricity without burning fossil fuels. Many countries are investing
in cleaner energy sources because they can reduce dependence on fuels that produce large
amounts of greenhouse gases. However, changing an energy system takes time, money,

tt LES 345) 7112م Gisenhoiie قز

(Egypt has also taken steps towards a greener future. Large solar and wind energy

individuals share responsibility, a more sustainable future becomes a realistic goal.

projects can contribute to cleaner electricity production, while other initiatives aim to improve
energy efficiency and reduce environmental damage. Such projects show that environmental
action is not only a global responsibility; it can also be part of a country’s development plans.

Large projects, however, are only part of the solution. Individuals can also make choices
that reduce pressure on the environment. Saving electricity, avoiding unnecessary waste,
using resources carefully, and choosing environmentally responsible products can all
contribute to change. One person’s action may seem small, but millions of people making
similar choices can have a significant effect.

Communities can make an even greater difference when people work together. Schools
can organize environmental campaigns, neighbourhoods can improve waste collection, and
local organizations can encourage tree planting or energy-saving projects. Cooperation can
turn individual efforts into larger and more lasting results.

The climate crisis is a serious challenge, but it is not a reason for people to feel powerless.
Scientific knowledge, renewable energy, government projects, and responsible individual
actions can all contribute to solutions. The most effective approach is to combine these
efforts rather than depend on one group alone.

Protecting the planet is therefore not simply about changing the future; it is about
changing decisions made today. When governments, organizations, communities, and

◆ الملخص العام | General Summary

Edward Malone is a young reporter working for the Daily Gazette in London. He loves
Gladys Hungerton and asks her to marry him, but she rejects him because she wants a
brave man who has faced danger. She tells him that true courage comes from a person's
character. Her words inspire Malone to search for adventure and prove himself.
لندن. يحب جلاديس هنجرتون ويطلب منها الزواج. لكنها ترفضه لأنها تريد iDaily Gazette إدوارد مالون صحفي شاب يعمل في جريدة
شجاعًا واجه الخطر. وتخبره أن الشجاعة الحقيقية تأتي من شخصية الإنسان. تلهم كلماتها مالون للبحث عن المغامرة وإثبات نفسه. tay
Malone asks his editor, Mr McArdle, for a dangerous assignment. McArdle sends him to
investigate Professor Challenger, a brilliant but aggressive zoologist who claims to have
discovered unknown prehistoric animals in South America. Challenger has photographs
and strange evidence but refuses to reveal the location. Most people doubt his story, so
Malone is asked to find the truth.
يطلب مالون من رئيس تحريره؛ السيد ماكأردل. مهمة خطيرة. ويرسله ماكأردل للتحقيق مع البروفيسور تشالنجر. وهو عالم حيوان عبقري لكنه
عدواني. ويدعي أنه اكتشف حيوانات مجهولة من عصور ما قبل التاريغ في أمريكا الجنوبية. يمتلك تشالنجر صورا وأدلة غريبة لكنه يرفض
With help from Tarp Henry, Malone pretends to be a young student and contacts
Challenger. Challenger accepts his letter and invites him to his house. Mrs Challenger
warns Malone that her husband is difficult and can become angry quickly. Malone meets
Challenger and finds him powerful, confident, and intimidating. Later, Malone joins
Challenger, Professor Summerlee, and Lord John Roxton on an expedition to South
America.
تشالنجر والبروفيسور سمرلي واللورد جون روكستون في رحلة استكشافية إلى أمريكا الجنوبية.
The explorers travel up the Amazon on the Esmeralda, then continue by canoe and finally
on foot. The journey is difficult because of the thick forest, bamboo, rocky slopes, and
dangerous conditions. They see a huge flying creature that Challenger identifies as a
pterodactyl. Later, they reach the cliffs drawn by Maple White and search for a way to
reach the plateau.
ثم يواصلون الرحلة بالزوارق وأخيراً سيرًا على الأقدام. وتكون الرحلة صعبة بسبب Esmeralda يسافر المستكشفون عبر الأمازون على متن
الغابات الكثيفة والخيزران والمنحدرات الصخرية والظروف الخطيرة. ويرون مخلوقًا طائرًا ضخمًا يحدده تشالنجر بأنه تيروداكتيل( زاحف مجنح).
وبعد ذلك يصلون إلى المنحدرات التي رسمها مابل وايت ويبحثون عن طريقة للوصول إلى الهضبة.
They discover a tree growing on a separate rock tower and use it as a bridge across a
deep chasm. After everyone crosses, Gomez appears and reveals that Pedro Lopez was
his brother and that he wants revenge against Lord John. Gomez destroys the tree bridge
and traps the explorers on the plateau. Fortunately, Zambo remains loyal and promises to
help them from below.
كان شقيقه وأنه يريد الانتقام من اللورد جون. ويدمر جوميز جسر الشجرة ويحاصر المستكشفين فوق الهضبة. ولحسن الحظ؛ يظل زامبو
ويتعهد بمساعدتهم من الأسفل. Lalie

[ ORI م The LOST WORLD

The group builds Fort Challenger and begins exploring the Lost World. They discover
enormous iguanodons, providing strong evidence that Challenger was telling the truth.
Summerlee finally accepts Challenger's claims. They also find a swamp filled with
hundreds of pterodactyls. The creatures attack them, injuring Malone, tearing Lord John's
coat, and knocking Challenger down. Lord John uses his rifle to help them escape.

تبني المجموعة Fort Challenger وتبدأ في استكشاف العالم المفقود. ويكتشفون إغوانودونات ضخمة (ديناصورات آكلة للنباتات)؛ مما يقدم
When they return to camp, they find that something has damaged their food and
ammunition. They cannot identify what entered the camp, but later notice strange
shadows, sounds, and tracks. These signs suggest that another unknown creature or even
people may live on the plateau. At the end of Chapter Five, Malone climbs a tree and

sees a large lake and smoke rising near some caves. The smoke suggests that someone or
something is living there, creating a new mystery.

عندما يعودون إلى المعسكر. يجدون أن شيئًا ما أتلف الطعام والذخيرة. ولا يستطيعون معرفة Le الذي دخل المعسكر. لكنهم يلاحظون لاحقًا
dts وأصوانً وآثارًا غريبة. وتشير هذه العلامات إلى احتمال وجود مخلوق مجهول آخر أو حتى أشخاص يعيشون فوق الهضبة. وفي النهاية ؛

For the first time, I saw more than the rude, explosive man described by the newspapers.
Challenger was proud and difficult, but he was also completely certain of what he had seen.
| believed that he believed his story. More than that, I was beginning to believe it too. Before I
left, he told me about a public lecture at the Zoological Institute the following evening. A well-
known speaker would discuss the history of life on Earth. Challenger expected his South
American claims to be mentioned. "Attend if you wish," he said. "You may find the evening
educational."

The animals seemed less afraid of humans the farther we traveled. Then the water became
too shallow. We hid the canoes and continued on foot, 007/09 food, rifles, blankets,
instruments, and medical supplies.

bamboo, and wide areas of tree-ferns. Progress was exhausting. Sometimes we spent an

ahead. Challenger and Summerlee argued constantly. "We should follow the compass,"
Challenger insisted one morning.

"There may be people here," I said. "Or something close to people,” Challenger replied.
Summerlee frowned. "Let us not create a theory before we have evidence." It was good
advice. Yet the forest seemed to be giving us a warming.

One afternoon | climbed a high tree at the edge of a clearing. From the upper branches 1)
hoped to see more of the plateau and perhaps discover a possible escape route. The view
was astonishing. The plateau was far larger than I had imagined. Forest covered most of it,
but in the center lay a broad lake shining in the sun. On the far side I could make out a line of
cliffs with dark openings that might be caves.
في أحد أيام بعد الظهر. تسلقت شجرة عالية عند حافة منطقة مكشوفة. وكنت آمل. من بين أغصانها العليا. أن أرى مساحة أكبر من الهضبة. وربما اكتشف
للخروج. كان المنظر مذهنا. كانت الهضبة أكبر بكثير مما تغيلت. وكانت الغابة تغطي معظمها. لكن في وسطلها كانت هناك بحيرة واسعة تلمع Laine طريقًا

نحت أشعة الشمس. وعلى الجانب البعيد. استطعت أن أميز صفًا من المنحدرات تتخللها فتحات مظلمة قد تكون كهوفًا. نا

Then | noticed something else. A thin line of smoke rose near those caves. Snoke meant
fire. And fire usually meant people. | climbed down with my mind racing. The lost world was
not only a land of prehistoric animals. Someone - or something - was living there too.
ثم لاحظت شينا آخر. كان خيط رفيع من الدخان يتصاعد بالقرب من تلك الكهوف. والدخان يعني وجود نار. والنار تعني عادة وجود أناس. نزلت من الشجرة
